The company specializes in EHR/EMR software designed specifically for **procedure-based specialties**, with its roots in endoscopy documentation and image capture. EndoSoft is a subsidiary of Utech Products Inc. and employs approximately 50–100 people.\n     9→\n    10→EndoSoft claims over 100,000 clinical users worldwide across 100+ hospitals, with operations in 40+ countries including the US, UK, Germany, Australia, India, China, UAE, and Canada. The company maintains international offices in the Netherlands, the UK, Australia, India, China, Germany, and the UAE. Their primary market is endoscopy suites, ambulatory surgery centers, and procedure-focused specialty practices, though they have expanded into oncology and other specialties.\n    11→\n    12→The company's product lineup includes EndoVault (the flagship certified EHR), Qlinical (a cloud-based EHR alternative), Argus AI (AI-powered documentation), and Approvon (a clinical vetting/workflow management application for the UK market). EndoVault is the ONC-certified product and the focus of this research.\n    13→\n    14→## Product: EndoVault\n    15→\n    16→CHPL IDs: 10854\n    17→\n    18→### What It Is\n    19→\n    20→EndoVault is an ONC-certified, multi-specialty EHR designed for procedure-focused medical specialties. While the product supports many specialties, its core strength and origin is in **gastroenterology/endoscopy** — it was originally built to capture and document endoscopic procedures with integrated imaging. The product has since expanded to support oncology, pulmonology, pain management, general surgery, orthopedics, OB/GYN, otolaryngology (ENT), urology, pathology, ophthalmology, dermatology, and cardiology.\n    21→\n    22→The certified module appears to be the full EndoVault EHR platform, not a subset. The CHPL certification covers 41+ criteria spanning clinical documentation (a), transitions of care (b), clinical quality measures (c), security (d), patient portal (e), public health reporting (f), and API/FHIR access (g). Intended users per the certification are \"Physicians, Nurses, Medical Technicians, Clerical Staff and Non-Clinical staff.\"\n    23→\n    24→EndoVault runs on Microsoft SQL Server and Interbase database platforms. It is described as scope-manufacturer-neutral, working with Olympus, Pentax, Fujinon, Stryker, and other scope brands.\n    25→\n    26→### Users & Market\n    27→\n    28→EndoVault serves procedure-focused practices and facilities including:\n    29→- **Gastroenterology/endoscopy suites** (the primary market)\n    30→- **Ambulatory surgery centers (ASCs)**\n    31→- **Hospital-based endoscopy and procedure units**\n    32→- **Oncology clinics** (chemotherapy management, cancer registry)\n    33→- **Multi-specialty procedure practices**\n    34→\n    35→Day-to-day users include gastroenterologists, endoscopists, oncologists, proceduralists, endoscopy nurses, medical technicians, scheduling/admitting staff, and practice managers. The ENR (Electronic Nursing Record) module is specifically designed for procedural nursing workflows.\n    36→\n    37→Notable customers include McGill University's Segal Cancer Centre and Queen Elisabeth Hospital (UK). In Australia, EndoVault integrates with the National Bowel Cancer Screening Program (NBCSP) and the National Cancer Screening Register (NCSR).\n    38→\n    39→On Capterra, EndoVault has a 4.0/5 rating based on 1,284 reviews. lower 3/5 based on only 2 reviews.\n    40→\n    41→### Modules & Functionality\n    42→\n    43→EndoVault is a modular system with the following components described across vendor materials:\n materials and the feature descriptions above, EndoVault stores and manages the following categories of data:\n   150→\n   151→**Clinical Data**: Patient demographics, medical history, allergies, medications, problem lists, diagnoses (ICD-O-3, TNM staging for oncology), vital signs (automatically captured from monitors), procedure findings, clinical assessments, progress notes, consult reports, discharge summaries.\n   152→\n   153→**Procedure Documentation**: Detailed procedure reports with findings (e.g., polyp characteristics, cecal intubation, bowel prep quality), intra-procedure nursing documentation (sedation scores, IV access), post-procedure observations (pain, consciousness, adverse events).\n   154→\n   155→**Images & Video**: Still images (BMP, JPEG, TIF, DICOM) and video recordings (AVI, up to 4K) from endoscopic and other procedures, stored in integrated PACS with DICOM compliance. This is a core data type for this product — it was originally built around endoscopy image capture.\n   156→\n   157→**Orders**: CPOE data for medications, labs, and imaging. Chemotherapy orders with dosing calculations, cycle tracking, and medication administration records.\n   158→\n   159→**Prescriptions**: Electronic prescriptions via Surescripts integration.\n   160→\n   161→**Pathology**: Pathology requisitions and results, tissue sample tracking.\n   162→\n   163→**Scheduling**: Appointment data, recalls, multi-provider/multi-facility schedules, patient admitting information.\n   164→\n   165→**Patient Portal Data**: Patient-submitted questionnaires and forms, electronic messages between patients and providers, lab results shared with patients.\n   166→\n   167→**Inventory & Equipment**: Scope tracking data (infection prevention), RFID/barcode inventory records, time and material billing data (items used per procedure, room utilization).\n   168→\n   169→**Quality Metrics**: Registry data for GIQuIC, AGA, MIPS/MACRA, CDC, NBCSP. Adenoma detection rates, polyp detection rates, bowel prep scores, and other quality indicators.\n   170→\n   171→**Billing Data**: Time and material billing is integrated in the ENR module, tracking items used and room utilization. However, full practice management/billing appears to be handled via interfaces with external billing systems — the vendor website states EndoSoft \"has interfaced with all billing and scheduling software vendors for all major Hospital Information systems.\" This suggests billing claims processing is **not** built into EndoVault itself, but billing data (charges, items, time) is generated within the system and transmitted outbound.\n   172→\n   173→**Audit/Compliance**: Audit trails documenting all chart modifications, electronic signatures for consent and procedure sign-offs, multi-factor authentication logs.\n   174→\n   175→**Oncology-Specific Data**: Cancer staging, chemotherapy regimens and cycles, radiation therapy orders, adverse reaction histories, lifetime medication restrictions, tumor board records, clinical trial consent, cancer registry submissions.\n   176→\n   177→**Notable Gaps/Uncertainty**: The vendor website does not describe a built-in full practice management or revenue cycle management system. Billing appears limited to time-and-materials tracking within procedures, with claims/revenue cycle handled externally. However, full practice management/billing appears to be handled via interfaces with external billing systems — the vendor website states EndoSoft \"has interfaced with all billing and scheduling software vendors for all major Hospital Information systems.\" This suggests billing claims processing is **not** built into EndoVault itself, but billing data (charges, items, time) is generated within the system and transmitted outbound.\n\n**Audit/Compliance**: Audit trails documenting all chart modifications, electronic signatures for consent and procedure sign-offs, multi-factor authentication logs.\n\n**Oncology-Specific Data**: Cancer staging, chemotherapy regimens and cycles, radiation therapy orders, adverse reaction histories, lifetime medication restrictions, tumor board records, clinical trial consent, cancer registry submissions.\n\n**Notable Gaps/Uncertainty**: The vendor website does not describe a built-in full practice management or revenue cycle management system. (ePHI) Export EHI – Electronic protected health information (ePHI) Export functionality allows EndoVault to do an export of health data for one or more patients. EndoVault supports the data export in CCD/C-CDA and ndjson formats. The C-CDA file uses stylesheets which is native to EndoSoft and provides a better user experience in viewing the patient data. End-user can define what kind of data that they want to export. Below are the available sections that a user can choose. Allergies Encounter Immunizations Medications Plan of treatment Referral reason Active problems Reason for visit Implants Health concerns Procedures Functional status Results Social history Vitals Goals Discharge instructions Assessments Cognitive status Media Diagnostic Report Documents Service Request EndoVault offers two methods for data export. Single/multi patient export Bulk EHI export Single/multi patient export EndoVault uses   HL7 C-CDA 2.1   guidelines to export the data. The file name format would be. File Name Description *.xml Discrete data elements CDA.xsl Stylesheet EndoVault supports both “All patient information” as well as “Single patient information” data export. Bulk EHI Export EndoVault uses FHIR APIs to export the data. More information can be found   here . Orthopedics OB/GYN Otolaryngology Pathology Urology Company About Us Locations Leadership Certifications & Memberships Careers Frequently Asked Questions News Patents Events Contact Us Support EHI (ePHI) Export EHI – Electronic protected health information (ePHI) Export functionality allows EndoVault to do an export of health data for one or more patients. EndoVault supports the data export in CCD/C-CDA and ndjson formats. The C-CDA file uses stylesheets which is native to EndoSoft and provides a better user experience in viewing the patient data. End-user can define what kind of data that they want to export. Below are the available sections that a user can choose. Allergies Encounter Immunizations Medications Plan of treatment Referral reason Active problems Reason for visit Implants Health concerns Procedures Functional status Results Social history Vitals Goals Discharge instructions Assessments Cognitive status Media Diagnostic Report Documents Service Request EndoVault offers two methods for data export. Single/multi patient export Bulk EHI export Single/multi patient export EndoVault uses   HL7 C-CDA 2.1   guidelines to export the data. The file name format would be. File Name Description *.xml Discrete data elements CDA.xsl Stylesheet EndoVault supports both “All patient information” as well as “Single patient information” data export. Bulk EHI Export EndoVault uses FHIR APIs to export the data. More information can be found   here . mega-menu-item-4499\" id=\"mega-menu-item-4499\"><a class=\"mega-menu-link\" href=\"https://www.endosoft.com/patents/\">Patents</a></li><li class=\"mega-menu-item mega-menu-item-type-custom mega-menu-item-object-custom mega-menu-item-858\" id=\"mega-menu-item-858\"><a class=\"mega-menu-link\" href=\"/events/\">Events</a></li></ul></li><li class=\"mega-menu-item mega-menu-item-type-custom mega-menu-item-object-custom mega-align-bottom-left mega-menu-flyout mega-menu-item-859\" id=\"mega-menu-item-859\"><a class=\"mega-menu-link\" href=\"/contact/\" tabindex=\"0\">Contact Us</a></li><li class=\"mega-menu-item mega-menu-item-type-custom mega-menu-item-object-custom mega-align-bottom-left mega-menu-flyout mega-menu-item-860\" id=\"mega-menu-item-860\"><a class=\"mega-menu-link\" href=\"/support\" tabindex=\"0\">Support</a></li></ul></div></nav></div></div></header><div id=\"content\" class=\"site-content clear\"><div id=\"primary\" class=\"content-area\"><main id=\"main\" class=\"site-main\"><article id=\"post-2318\" class=\"post-2318 page type-page status-publish has-post-thumbnail hentry\"><div id='hero' class='container-fluid'> <noscript><img src=\"/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/shutterstock_1015882696-2.webp\" alt=\"Decorative BG image: doctor preparing\" /></noscript><img class=\"lazyload\" src='data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns=%22http://www.w3.org/2000/svg%22%20viewBox=%220%200%20210%20140%22%3E%3C/svg%3E' data-src=\"/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/shutterstock_1015882696-2.webp\" alt=\"Decorative BG image: doctor preparing\" /><div class='row'><div class=\"row double-row\"><div class='col col-12'><h1>EHI (ePHI) Export</h1></div></div></div></div><div class=\"entry-content\"><div id=\"page-content\" class=\"container\"><div class=\"row\"><div class=\"wpb_text_column wpb_content_element starting\"><div class=\"wpb_wrapper\"><p>EHI – Electronic protected health information (ePHI) Export functionality allows EndoVault to do an export of health data for one or more patients. EndoVault supports the data export in CCD/C-CDA and ndjson formats. The C-CDA file uses stylesheets which is native to EndoSoft and provides a better user experience in viewing the patient data.</p></div></div><div class=\"wpb_text_column wpb_content_element \"><div class=\"wpb_wrapper\"><p>End-user can define what kind of data that they want to export. Below are the available sections that a user can choose.</p><ul><li>Allergies</li><li>Encounter</li><li>Immunizations</li><li>Medications</li><li>Plan of treatment</li><li>Referral reason</li><li>Active problems</li><li>Reason for visit</li><li>Implants</li><li>Health concerns</li><li>Procedures</li><li>Functional status</li><li>Results</li><li>Social history</li><li>Vitals</li><li>Goals</li><li>Discharge instructions</li><li>Assessments</li><li>Cognitive status</li><li>Media</li><li>Diagnostic Report</li><li>Documents</li><li>Service Request</li></ul></div></div><div class=\"wpb_text_column wpb_content_element \"><div class=\"wpb_wrapper\"><p>EndoVault offers two methods for data export.</p><ul><li>Single/multi patient export</li><li>Bulk EHI export</li></ul></div></div><div class=\"wpb_text_column wpb_content_element \"><div class=\"wpb_wrapper\"><p><strong><u>Single/multi patient export</u></strong></p><p>EndoVault uses<span> </span><span><a href=\"https://www.hl7.org/ccdasearch/\">HL7 C-CDA 2.1</a></span><span> </span>guidelines to export the data. The file name format would be.</p><table><tbody><tr><td><strong>File Name</strong></td><td><strong>Description</strong></td></tr><tr><td>*.xml</td><td>Discrete data elements</td></tr><tr><td>CDA.xsl</td><td>Stylesheet</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div><div class=\"wpb_text_column wpb_content_element \"><div class=\"wpb_wrapper\"><p>EndoVault supports both “All patient information” as well as “Single patient information” data export.</p><p><strong><u>Bulk EHI Export</u></strong></p><p>EndoVault uses FHIR APIs to export the data. More information can be found<span> </span><a href=\"https://old.endosoft.com/fhir/#_Toc120735483\"><span>here</span></a>.</p></div></div></div></div></div></article></main></div></div><footer EndoSoft ® FHIR API The EndoVault® SMART on FHIR is a service developed at EndoSoft ® for accessing the patient’s data through RESTful API. SMART is an open-source, standards-based API that leverages the OAuth 2.0 standard to provide secure, universal access to EHRs. The SMART platform builds on the existing Fast Health Interoperability Resources (hence the name &#8220;SMART on FHIR&#8221;). SMART standardizes the process through which a third-party application could plug into a data store and access that clinical information. FHIR provides a set of models to standardize the representation of clinical concepts such as allergies and medications in an EHR or other clinical data store. EndoSoft ® FHIR API The EndoVault® SMART on FHIR is a service developed at EndoSoft ® for accessing the patient’s data through RESTful API. SMART is an open-source, standards-based API that leverages the OAuth 2.0 standard to provide secure, universal access to EHRs. The SMART platform builds on the existing Fast Health Interoperability Resources (hence the name &#8220;SMART on FHIR&#8221;). SMART standardizes the process through which a third-party application could plug into a data store and access that clinical information. FHIR provides a set of models to standardize the representation of clinical concepts such as allergies and medications in an EHR or other clinical data store. SMART is an open-source, standards-based API that leverages the OAuth 2.0 standard to\nprovide secure, universal access to EHRs. The SMART platform builds on the existing Fast Health\nInteroperability Resources (hence the name \"SMART on FHIR\"). SMART standardizes the process through\nwhich a third-party application could plug into a data store and access that clinical information. FHIR\nprovides a set of models to standardize the representation of clinical concepts such as allergies and\nmedications in an EHR or other clinical data store.\nReferences:\n•\n\nhttps://inferno.healthit.gov/suites/g10_certification\n\nAuthorization\nThe model supports authorization and authentication by following Outh2 specifications. The benefit of\nusing authorization is that we can define the level of access that a client application can perform. It also\nbrings down the risk by providing a two communication between the server and client applications.\nWhen the server receives a request from the client, a token will be generated and given back. This token\ndoes have an expiry attached to it. For further access to the server, the client must provide the same\ntoken.\nReferences:\n•\n\nhttp://hl7.org/fhir/smart-app-launch/STU2/\n\nRegistration\nTo get the access to the resources, the client applications need to be registered with EndoSoft. During\nregistration process, a client code and password will be generated for the client which will be later used\nfor token generation.\n\nAPI Endpoint\nRepresentational State Transfer (REST) is a style of software architecture for distributed hypermedia\nsystems such as the World Wide Web. REST-style architectures consist of clients and servers. Clients\ninitiate requests to servers; servers process requests and return appropriate responses. Requests and\nresponses are built around the transfer of \"representations\" of \"resources\". A resource can be essentially\nany coherent and meaningful concept that may be addressed. A representation of a resource is typically\na document that captures the current or intended state of a resource. The EndoVault RESTful API is a\nsimple service implemented using HTTPS and can be thought of as a collection of resources, specified as\nURLs. Some characteristics of the EndoVault RESTful web API:\n•\n\nThe base URL for the web service is https://< service URL>\nCompany Confidential\n\n5\n\n\f•\n•\n•\n\nThe base URL for the bulk data transfer is https://<service URL>/bulk\nThe service can return the data in JSON format.\nThe service only supports HTTPS method GET and POST.\n\nClients of the EndoVault RESTful API can be any applications that support HTTPS POST.\nSecure channel can be tested from any web browser such as Internet Explorer, Google Chrome, or\nFirefox etc.\n\nScopes\nSecurity\nWell-known End point\nThe authorization endpoints accepted by a FHIR resource server can be exposed as a Well-Known\nUniform Resource Identifier.\nHTTP verb: GET\nSyntax: <base url> + /.well-known/smart-configuration\nParameters: No parameters required.\nAuthorization: No auth required\nExample\nhttps://fhirapi.endosoft.com/.well-known/smart-configuration\nName\naccept\ncontent-type\nHTTP verb\nParameters\nResponse","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0c754d0a-a2f0-48c7-bd3d-d9c83fa98052","uuid":"8cf1b968-cdcd-4917-8a3d-6ea7f4883f27","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"11/25/2022\n\nAPI Definition\nConfidentiality and Proprietary Rights\nThis document is the confidential property of EndoSoft LLC. ....................................................................................................... 138\n\nCompany Confidential\n\n4\n\n\fOverview\nThe EndoVault SMART on FHIR is a service developed at EndoSoft for accessing the patient’s data through\nRESTful API. SMART is an open-source, standards-based API that leverages the OAuth 2.0 standard to\nprovide secure, universal access to EHRs. The SMART platform builds on the existing Fast Health\nInteroperability Resources (hence the name \"SMART on FHIR\"). SMART standardizes the process through\nwhich a third-party application could plug into a data store and access that clinical information. FHIR\nprovides a set of models to standardize the representation of clinical concepts such as allergies and\nmedications in an EHR or other clinical data store.\nReferences:\n•\n\nhttps://inferno.healthit.gov/suites/g10_certification\n\nAuthorization\nThe model supports authorization and authentication by following Outh2 specifications. The benefit of\nusing authorization is that we can define the level of access that a client application can perform. It also\nbrings down the risk by providing a two communication between the server and client applications.\nWhen the server receives a request from the client, a token will be generated and given back. This token\ndoes have an expiry attached to it. For further access to the server, the client must provide the same\ntoken.\nReferences:\n•\n\nhttp://hl7.org/fhir/smart-app-launch/STU2/\n\nRegistration\nTo get the access to the resources, the client applications need to be registered with EndoSoft. During\nregistration process, a client code and password will be generated for the client which will be later used\nfor token generation.\n\nAPI Endpoint\nRepresentational State Transfer (REST) is a style of software architecture for distributed hypermedia\nsystems such as the World Wide Web. REST-style architectures consist of clients and servers. Clients\ninitiate requests to servers; servers process requests and return appropriate responses. Requests and\nresponses are built around the transfer of \"representations\" of \"resources\". A resource can be essentially\nany coherent and meaningful concept that may be addressed. A representation of a resource is typically\na document that captures the current or intended state of a resource. The EndoVault RESTful API is a\nsimple service implemented using HTTPS and can be thought of as a collection of resources, specified as\nURLs. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01Bv7D4KjgdVAjwQb9WKDntR","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Vpuf1CBAasmEsmucoyG4N9","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/endosoft-llc--endovault/ehi-export-report.md","content":"# EndoSoft, LLC — EHI Export Documentation\n\nCollected: 2026-02-15\n\n## Source\n- Registered URL: https://www.endosoft.com/ephi/\n- CHPL IDs: 10854\n- Product: EndoVault 3.2\n- Certification date: 2022-03-10\n\n## Navigation Journal\n\n### Step 1: Probe the registered URL\n```bash\ncurl -sI -L \"https://www.endosoft.com/ephi/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'\n```\nReturned HTTP 200, Content-Type: text/html. WordPress site. Page loaded successfully.\n\n### Step 2: Fetch and examine the EHI export page\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.endosoft.com/ephi/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o ephi-page.html\n```\nPage title: \"EndoSoft® ePHI - Secure Protected Health Information\". The page describes two EHI export methods:\n\n1. **Single/multi patient export** using HL7 C-CDA 2.1 guidelines (XML files + CDA.xsl stylesheet)\n2. **Bulk EHI Export** using FHIR APIs, with a link labeled \"here\" pointing to `https://old.endosoft.com/fhir/#_Toc120735483`\n\nThe page lists 22 data sections available for export: Allergies, Encounter, Immunizations, Medications, Plan of treatment, Referral reason, Active problems, Reason for visit, Implants, Health concerns, Procedures, Functional status, Results, Social history, Vitals, Goals, Discharge instructions, Assessments, Cognitive status, Media, Diagnostic Report, Documents, Service Request.\n\n### Step 3: Follow the \"here\" link for Bulk EHI Export\nThe link on the EHI page points to `https://old.endosoft.com/fhir/#_Toc120735483`. This URL is **broken**:\n- HTTPS returns `ERR_CERT_COMMON_NAME_INVALID` (invalid SSL certificate)\n- With `-k` (skip cert verification): returns HTTP 502 Bad Gateway\n- No Wayback Machine snapshots exist for `old.endosoft.com/fhir/`\n\n### Step 4: Find the working FHIR documentation\nDiscovered that `https://www.endosoft.com/fhir/` exists and returns HTTP 200. This page describes the EndoVault SMART on FHIR service and contains a \"Click Here to View FHIR API\" link pointing to a PDF:\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.endosoft.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/170.315-g10-Standardized-API-FHIR-2.pdf\" -o 170.315-g10-Standardized-API-FHIR-2.pdf\n```\nThe PDF downloaded successfully — 138 pages, 1.3 MB, titled \"API Definition\" dated 11/25/2022 by EndoSoft LLC.\n\n### Step 5: Check the mandatory disclosures page\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.endosoft.com/mu_stage3/\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0'\n```\nThe page confirms certification for 170.315 (b)(10) Electronic Health Information Export along with all other criteria. Contains a cost disclosure link:\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.endosoft.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/endovault_ehr_disclosure_onc_2015-10_15_25.xlsx\" -o endovault_ehr_disclosure_onc_2015-10_15_25.xlsx\n```\nDownloaded successfully (23 KB XLSX file).\n\n### Step 6: Examine the FHIR API PDF\nThe 138-page PDF covers the full EndoVault SMART on FHIR API, including:\n- Authorization (OAuth 2.0, SMART on FHIR)\n- Individual patient API endpoints (pages 5-119)\n- **Bulk Data Access section** (pages 120-132)\n- EndoVault API resources table (page 134)\n- API Rate and Quota (page 135)\n- Examples (page 135)\n- Terms of Service (pages 136-138)\n\n## What Was Found\n\nEndoSoft documents two EHI export mechanisms:\n\n### 1. Single/Multi-Patient C-CDA Export\nThe EHI page states that EndoVault exports patient data in CCD/C-CDA format using HL7 C-CDA 2.1 guidelines. The export produces:\n- `*.xml` files containing discrete data elements\n- `CDA.xsl` stylesheet for rendering\n\nUsers can select from 22 named data sections to include in the export. The documentation for this method is limited to the brief description on the `/ephi/` page itself — there is no separate data dictionary, schema, or detailed specification for the C-CDA export format.\n\n### 2. Bulk EHI Export via FHIR API\nThe Bulk EHI Export uses the same FHIR API documented in the 138-page PDF (`170.315-g10-Standardized-API-FHIR-2.pdf`). The Bulk Data section (pages 120-132) describes static NDJSON file endpoints for 18 FHIR resource types:\n\n| Resource | Bulk Endpoint |\n|----------|--------------|\n| AllergyIntolerance | `/bulk/allergyintolerance-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| CarePlan | `/bulk/careplan-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| CareTeam | `/bulk/careteam-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Condition | `/bulk/condition-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Device | `/bulk/device-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| DiagnosticReport | `/bulk/diagnosticreport-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| DocumentReference | `/bulk/documentreference-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Encounter | `/bulk/encounter-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Goal | `/bulk/goal-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Immunization | `/bulk/immunization-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| MedicationRequest | `/bulk/medicationrequest-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Observation | `/bulk/observation-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Organization | `/bulk/organization-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Practitioner | `/bulk/practitioner-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Procedure | `/bulk/procedure-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Provenance | `/bulk/provenance-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| RelatedPerson | `/bulk/relatedperson-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| ServiceRequest | `/bulk/servicerequest-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n\nEach resource endpoint is documented with a sample JSON response. The samples are generally well-formed FHIR R4 resources using standard US Core profiles.\n\nThe individual patient API section covers the same 18 resource types with more detailed query parameters and search capabilities per resource.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nEndoVault is a specialty EHR focused on procedure-based specialties (primarily gastroenterology/endoscopy, with extensions into oncology, pulmonology, and others). Comparing the product research to the documented export:\n\n**Clearly covered (via FHIR resources and C-CDA sections):**\n- Demographics (Patient)\n- Allergies (AllergyIntolerance)\n- Medications (MedicationRequest)\n- Diagnoses / Problem lists (Condition)\n- Immunizations (Immunization)\n- Procedures (Procedure)\n- Lab results, vitals, clinical observations (Observation — the PDF has extensive documentation spanning pages 45-103 covering vital signs, lab results, smoking status, pulse ox, and social determinants)\n- Care plans (CarePlan)\n- Care teams (CareTeam)\n- Goals (Goal)\n- Encounters (Encounter)\n- Diagnostic reports (DiagnosticReport)\n- Documents / clinical notes (DocumentReference)\n- Implantable devices (Device)\n- Service requests / orders (ServiceRequest)\n- Related persons (RelatedPerson)\n- Provenance tracking (Provenance)\n\n**Missing or not mentioned — significant gaps given the product's specialty focus:**\n- **Endoscopy images and video** — This is arguably the most critical data type for EndoVault. The product was built around HD endoscopic image capture (BMP, JPEG, TIF, DICOM) and video recording (AVI, 4K). The FHIR export includes \"Media\" as a C-CDA section name but the Bulk Data API does not include a Media resource endpoint. There is a DocumentReference resource that could theoretically reference images, but the sample data shows only clinical notes, not images. The export documentation does not address how the product's core imaging data is exported.\n- **Procedure-specific endoscopy findings** — EndoVault stores rich specialty data: polyp characteristics, cecal intubation records, bowel prep quality scores (Boston scale), adenoma detection data, scope tracking. None of this specialty data is addressed in the FHIR export documentation. It is unclear whether this data is captured in Observation or Procedure resources, or omitted entirely.\n- **Oncology-specific data** — Cancer staging (TNM, ICD-O-3), chemotherapy regimens, radiation therapy orders, adverse reaction histories, lifetime medication restrictions, tumor board records, clinical trial consent. None of these are mentioned in the export.\n- **Electronic Nursing Record (ENR) data** — Vital sign recordings from monitors during procedures, medication administration during procedures, sedation scores, IV assessments, pain scales, adverse events, consciousness levels, discharge status. The Observation resource may capture some vitals, but the procedural nursing workflow data is not addressed.\n- **Pathology data** — Pathology requisitions and results are a named module in EndoVault but have no specific export representation beyond what may be in DiagnosticReport.\n- **Billing/charge data** — Time and material billing data (items used per procedure, room utilization) is generated in EndoVault. There is no billing-related FHIR resource in the export.\n- **Scheduling/recall data** — While scheduling is operational data (not necessarily EHI), recall management for follow-up procedures (e.g., colonoscopy surveillance) could be clinically relevant if it documents care recommendations.\n- **Patient portal data** — Patient-submitted questionnaires and electronic messages. Not addressed.\n- **E-prescriptions** — Surescripts prescriptions are mentioned in the product but MedicationRequest only captures medication orders, not the full e-prescribing transaction.\n- **Quality metrics** — Registry data (GIQuIC, AGA) and quality indicators. These are aggregated metrics and may not qualify as EHI, but individual patient quality data (e.g., a specific patient's adenoma detection) would be.\n- **PACS/DICOM images** — The integrated PACS with DICOM-compliant storage is a core feature. Export of DICOM images is not documented.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses two recognized standards:\n1. **C-CDA 2.1** (HL7) for single/multi-patient export — a well-established clinical document format\n2. **FHIR R4 NDJSON** for bulk export — using what appear to be standard US Core profiles\n\nBoth are appropriate standards for clinical data exchange. However, the FHIR bulk export appears to be a repackaging of the (g)(10) standardized API as the (b)(10) export. The evidence:\n\n- The PDF is titled \"170.315-g10-Standardized-API-FHIR-2.pdf\" — it is explicitly the g(10) documentation\n- The resource types (18 total) map precisely to the US Core resource types required by g(10)\n- There are no EndoVault-custom FHIR profiles or extensions for specialty data\n- No resource types exist for the product's core differentiators (endoscopy images, procedure findings, oncology data)\n- The Bulk Data endpoints are simple static NDJSON file downloads, not a true Bulk Data Export API with kick-off/status/download workflow\n\nThe C-CDA export's 22 named sections align with standard C-CDA sections and include some additions (Media, Diagnostic Report, Documents, Service Request) but there is no documentation of how specialty data maps to these sections.\n\n**A third party could reconstruct a basic clinical summary** (demographics, problems, meds, allergies, labs, vitals, encounters) from the export, but could **not** reconstruct the complete patient record as stored in EndoVault. The endoscopy procedure documentation, imaging, oncology data, and procedural nursing records — the product's primary clinical value — are not covered.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n- **Readability**: The EHI export page itself is clear and well-organized, though very brief. The 138-page FHIR API PDF is reasonably structured with a table of contents and resource-by-resource documentation.\n- **Data dictionary**: There is no field-level data dictionary. The FHIR API PDF provides sample JSON responses for each resource type, which implicitly show the data model. However, these are standard FHIR resource structures — there is no EndoVault-specific mapping documentation showing which EndoVault database fields map to which FHIR elements.\n- **Data types and value sets**: Relies entirely on standard FHIR data types and US Core value set bindings. No EndoVault-specific coded value documentation.\n- **Worked examples**: The PDF includes sample JSON responses for each resource type and bulk endpoint, plus three example API calls with base64-encoded parameters.\n- **Developer implementability**: A developer familiar with FHIR could implement an import of the exported data. However, they would only receive the US Core clinical summary subset, not the full EndoVault dataset.\n- **Maintenance**: The PDF was authored 2022-12-12 and last modified the same day. The EHI page was last modified 2025-03-25. The cost disclosure XLSX is dated October 2025. The documentation appears to be maintained but the FHIR API PDF has not been updated since initial certification.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Field-level granularity**: The bulk data section documents each resource type with a sample JSON response. The individual API section (pages 15-119) provides more detail on query parameters and response structures per resource. However, there are no field-by-field descriptions, cardinality constraints, or required vs. optional designations beyond what FHIR US Core defines.\n- **Coded fields**: Standard FHIR code systems are used (SNOMED, LOINC, ICD-10). No EndoVault-specific code sets are documented.\n- **Relationships**: FHIR references between resources are shown in sample data (e.g., Encounter references in Conditions, Patient references everywhere). No entity-relationship diagram.\n- **Versioning**: No versioning or change history beyond the PDF creation date.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nEndoSoft's EHI export documentation reveals a classic (b)(10)/(g)(10) conflation. The \"Bulk EHI Export\" is the same FHIR API used for g(10) certification, repackaged as the b(10) mechanism. This covers the US Core / USCDI clinical data subset but misses the product's core value proposition and specialty data:\n\n1. **Endoscopy imaging and video** — the product's original raison d'être — has no documented export path\n2. **Specialty procedure documentation** (polyp findings, bowel prep scores, cecal intubation) — not addressed\n3. **Oncology data** (staging, chemo, radiation) — not addressed\n4. **Procedural nursing records** (ENR) — not addressed\n5. **Billing/charge data** — not addressed\n6. **Pathology data** — not specifically addressed\n\nThe C-CDA single-patient export may capture slightly more than the FHIR bulk export (it lists 22 sections including \"Media\"), but there is no detailed documentation of what \"Media\" includes or how specialty data maps to C-CDA sections.\n\nThe documentation is functional for its g(10) purpose but inadequate as b(10) EHI export documentation. A patient or third party requesting their complete health information from an EndoVault system would receive a clinical summary but would be missing their endoscopy images, procedure reports with detailed findings, and any oncology treatment records — data that is clearly part of the designated record set and used for clinical decision-making.\n\nAn additional operational concern: the \"here\" link on the primary EHI export page (`/ephi/`) points to `old.endosoft.com/fhir/` which is completely broken (SSL cert error + 502 gateway). A user following the documented path would hit a dead end. The working FHIR page exists at `www.endosoft.com/fhir/` but the EHI page hasn't been updated to point there.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.endosoft.com/ephi/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (curl works fine)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page + one link to FHIR page/PDF)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n1. **Broken FHIR documentation link**: The EHI page links to `https://old.endosoft.com/fhir/#_Toc120735483` which returns SSL certificate error and 502 Bad Gateway. No Wayback Machine snapshots exist. The equivalent content is available at `https://www.endosoft.com/fhir/` (same domain, without \"old\" subdomain).\n\n2. **No dedicated b(10) documentation**: There is no document specifically addressing the b(10) EHI export requirement. The PDF is explicitly a g(10) FHIR API document. The EHI page provides only a brief overview without technical depth.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":681,"cache_read_input_tokens":87780,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":681},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0c754d0a-a2f0-48c7-bd3d-d9c83fa98052","uuid":"d001d08d-8c84-4f2a-9f8c-6b5422c63eb1"}

Bulk EHI Export via FHIR API\nThe Bulk EHI Export uses the same FHIR API documented in the 138-page PDF (`170.315-g10-Standardized-API-FHIR-2.pdf`). The Bulk Data section (pages 120-132) describes static NDJSON file endpoints for 18 FHIR resource types:\n\n| Resource | Bulk Endpoint |\n|----------|--------------|\n| AllergyIntolerance | `/bulk/allergyintolerance-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| CarePlan | `/bulk/careplan-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| CareTeam | `/bulk/careteam-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Condition | `/bulk/condition-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Device | `/bulk/device-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| DiagnosticReport | `/bulk/diagnosticreport-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| DocumentReference | `/bulk/documentreference-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Encounter | `/bulk/encounter-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Goal | `/bulk/goal-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Immunization | `/bulk/immunization-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| MedicationRequest | `/bulk/medicationrequest-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Observation | `/bulk/observation-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Organization | `/bulk/organization-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Practitioner | `/bulk/practitioner-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Procedure | `/bulk/procedure-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| Provenance | `/bulk/provenance-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| RelatedPerson | `/bulk/relatedperson-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n| ServiceRequest | `/bulk/servicerequest-bulkfile.ndjson` |\n\nEach resource endpoint is documented with a sample JSON response. The samples are generally well-formed FHIR R4 resources using standard US Core profiles.\n\nThe individual patient API section covers the same 18 resource types with more detailed query parameters and search capabilities per resource.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nEndoVault is a specialty EHR focused on procedure-based specialties (primarily gastroenterology/endoscopy, with extensions into oncology, pulmonology, and others). Comparing the product research to the documented export:\n\n**Clearly covered (via FHIR resources and C-CDA sections):**\n- Demographics (Patient)\n- Allergies (AllergyIntolerance)\n- Medications (MedicationRequest)\n- Diagnoses / Problem lists (Condition)\n- Immunizations (Immunization)\n- Procedures (Procedure)\n- Lab results, vitals, clinical observations (Observation — the PDF has extensive documentation spanning pages 45-103 covering vital signs, lab results, smoking status, pulse ox, and social determinants)\n- Care plans (CarePlan)\n- Care teams (CareTeam)\n- Goals (Goal)\n- Encounters (Encounter)\n- Diagnostic reports (DiagnosticReport)\n- Documents / clinical notes (DocumentReference)\n- Implantable devices (Device)\n- Service requests / orders (ServiceRequest)\n- Related persons (RelatedPerson)\n- Provenance tracking (Provenance)\n\n**Missing or not mentioned — significant gaps given the product's specialty focus:**\n- **Endoscopy images and video** — This is arguably the most critical data type for EndoVault. The product was built around HD endoscopic image capture (BMP, JPEG, TIF, DICOM) and video recording (AVI, 4K). The FHIR export includes \"Media\" as a C-CDA section name but the Bulk Data API does not include a Media resource endpoint. There is a DocumentReference resource that could theoretically reference images, but the sample data shows only clinical notes, not images. The export documentation does not address how the product's core imaging data is exported.\n- **Procedure-specific endoscopy findings** — EndoVault stores rich specialty data: polyp characteristics, cecal intubation records, bowel prep quality scores (Boston scale), adenoma detection data, scope tracking. None of this specialty data is addressed in the FHIR export documentation. It is unclear whether this data is captured in Observation or Procedure resources, or omitted entirely.\n- **Oncology-specific data** — Cancer staging (TNM, ICD-O-3), chemotherapy regimens, radiation therapy orders, adverse reaction histories, lifetime medication restrictions, tumor board records, clinical trial consent. None of these are mentioned in the export.\n- **Electronic Nursing Record (ENR) data** — Vital sign recordings from monitors during procedures, medication administration during procedures, sedation scores, IV assessments, pain scales, adverse events, consciousness levels, discharge status. The Observation resource may capture some vitals, but the procedural nursing workflow data is not addressed.\n- **Pathology data** — Pathology requisitions and results are a named module in EndoVault but have no specific export representation beyond what may be in DiagnosticReport.\n- **Billing/charge data** — Time and material billing data (items used per procedure, room utilization) is generated in EndoVault. There is no billing-related FHIR resource in the export.\n- **Scheduling/recall data** — While scheduling is operational data (not necessarily EHI), recall management for follow-up procedures (e.g., colonoscopy surveillance) could be clinically relevant if it documents care recommendations.\n- **Patient portal data** — Patient-submitted questionnaires and electronic messages. Not addressed.\n- **E-prescriptions** — Surescripts prescriptions are mentioned in the product but MedicationRequest only captures medication orders, not the full e-prescribing transaction.\n- **Quality metrics** — Registry data (GIQuIC, AGA) and quality indicators. These are aggregated metrics and may not qualify as EHI, but individual patient quality data (e.g., a specific patient's adenoma detection) would be.\n- **PACS/DICOM images** — The integrated PACS with DICOM-compliant storage is a core feature. Export of DICOM images is not documented.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses two recognized standards:\n1. **C-CDA 2.1** (HL7) for single/multi-patient export — a well-established clinical document format\n2. **FHIR R4 NDJSON** for bulk export — using what appear to be standard US Core profiles\n\nBoth are appropriate standards for clinical data exchange. However, the FHIR bulk export appears to be a repackaging of the (g)(10) standardized API as the (b)(10) export. The evidence:\n\n- The PDF is titled \"170.315-g10-Standardized-API-FHIR-2.pdf\" — it is explicitly the g(10) documentation\n- The resource types (18 total) map precisely to the US Core resource types required by g(10)\n- There are no EndoVault-custom FHIR profiles or extensions for specialty data\n- No resource types exist for the product's core differentiators (endoscopy images, procedure findings, oncology data)\n- The Bulk Data endpoints are simple static NDJSON file downloads, not a true Bulk Data Export API with kick-off/status/download workflow\n\nThe C-CDA export's 22 named sections align with standard C-CDA sections and include some additions (Media, Diagnostic Report, Documents, Service Request) but there is no documentation of how specialty data maps to these sections.\n\n**A third party could reconstruct a basic clinical summary** (demographics, problems, meds, allergies, labs, vitals, encounters) from the export, but could **not** reconstruct the complete patient record as stored in EndoVault. The endoscopy procedure documentation, imaging, oncology data, and procedural nursing records — the product's primary clinical value — are not covered.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n- **Readability**: The EHI export page itself is clear and well-organized, though very brief. The 138-page FHIR API PDF is reasonably structured with a table of contents and resource-by-resource documentation.\n- **Data dictionary**: There is no field-level data dictionary. The FHIR API PDF provides sample JSON responses for each resource type, which implicitly show the data model. However, these are standard FHIR resource structures — there is no EndoVault-specific mapping documentation showing which EndoVault database fields map to which FHIR elements.\n- **Data types and value sets**: Relies entirely on standard FHIR data types and US Core value set bindings. No EndoVault-specific coded value documentation.\n- **Worked examples**: The PDF includes sample JSON responses for each resource type and bulk endpoint, plus three example API calls with base64-encoded parameters.\n- **Developer implementability**: A developer familiar with FHIR could implement an import of the exported data. However, they would only receive the US Core clinical summary subset, not the full EndoVault dataset.\n- **Maintenance**: The PDF was authored 2022-12-12 and last modified the same day. The EHI page was last modified 2025-03-25. The cost disclosure XLSX is dated October 2025. The documentation appears to be maintained but the FHIR API PDF has not been updated since initial certification.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Field-level granularity**: The bulk data section documents each resource type with a sample JSON response. The individual API section (pages 15-119) provides more detail on query parameters and response structures per resource. However, there are no field-by-field descriptions, cardinality constraints, or required vs. optional designations beyond what FHIR US Core defines.\n- **Coded fields**: Standard FHIR code systems are used (SNOMED, LOINC, ICD-10). No EndoVault-specific code sets are documented.\n- **Relationships**: FHIR references between resources are shown in sample data (e.g., Encounter references in Conditions, Patient references everywhere). No entity-relationship diagram.\n- **Versioning**: No versioning or change history beyond the PDF creation date.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nEndoSoft's EHI export documentation reveals a classic (b)(10)/(g)(10) conflation. The \"Bulk EHI Export\" is the same FHIR API used for g(10) certification, repackaged as the b(10) mechanism. This covers the US Core / USCDI clinical data subset but misses the product's core value proposition and specialty data:\n\n1. **Endoscopy imaging and video** — the product's original raison d'être — has no documented export path\n2. **Specialty procedure documentation** (polyp findings, bowel prep scores, cecal intubation) — not addressed\n3. **Oncology data** (staging, chemo, radiation) — not addressed\n4. **Procedural nursing records** (ENR) — not addressed\n5. **Billing/charge data** — not addressed\n6. **Pathology data** — not specifically addressed\n\nThe C-CDA single-patient export may capture slightly more than the FHIR bulk export (it lists 22 sections including \"Media\"), but there is no detailed documentation of what \"Media\" includes or how specialty data maps to C-CDA sections.\n\nThe documentation is functional for its g(10) purpose but inadequate as b(10) EHI export documentation. A patient or third party requesting their complete health information from an EndoVault system would receive a clinical summary but would be missing their endoscopy images, procedure reports with detailed findings, and any oncology treatment records — data that is clearly part of the designated record set and used for clinical decision-making.\n\nAn additional operational concern: the \"here\" link on the primary EHI export page (`/ephi/`) points to `old.endosoft.com/fhir/` which is completely broken (SSL cert error + 502 gateway). A user following the documented path would hit a dead end. The working FHIR page exists at `www.endosoft.com/fhir/` but the EHI page hasn't been updated to point there.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://www.endosoft.com/ephi/\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (curl works fine)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (main page + one link to FHIR page/PDF)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n\n1. **Broken FHIR documentation link**: The EHI page links to `https://old.endosoft.com/fhir/#_Toc120735483` which returns SSL certificate error and 502 Bad Gateway. No Wayback Machine snapshots exist. The equivalent content is available at `https://www.endosoft.com/fhir/` (same domain, without \"old\" subdomain).\n\n2. **No dedicated b(10) documentation**: There is no document specifically addressing the b(10) EHI export requirement. The PDF is explicitly a g(10) FHIR API document. The EHI page provides only a brief overview without technical depth.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
